🎓 Welcome to the complete Machine Learning with Python full course — absolutely free and beginner-friendly! In this in-depth course, you'll learn everything you need to get started with machine learning using Python, from the foundational concepts to advanced algorithms and real-world applications. We'll begin with a quick Python refresher and gradually move into core ML topics like supervised and unsupervised learning, model training and evaluation, data preprocessing, feature engineering, and more. You’ll work with popular Python libraries such as NumPy, Pandas, Matplotlib, Seaborn, and Scikit-learn, and by the end of the course, you’ll have hands-on experience building, testing, and even deploying machine learning models. Whether you're a student, a developer looking to pivot into AI/ML, or just curious about how machines learn from data — this course is for you. No prior experience in ML is needed, just basic Python knowledge. Stick around for bonus sections covering interview tips, career advice, and useful learning resources. Don’t forget to like, subscribe, and turn on notifications so you never miss an update. Let's dive into the world of Machine Learning together!

[394:37] I have data point which is over here.

[518:43] calculations happen till the point you

[519:56] So as you can see here in this slide we

[520:07] points. Now if you have a new data point

[521:10] therefore we will now classify it as

[521:21] So

[521:35] one needs to understand is that as the
[521:38] value of K increases basically the the
[521:42] partition line starts moving towards
[521:45] becoming more and more linear. So it
[521:47] starts becoming less flexible and it
[521:49] starts assuming some kind of a linear
[521:51] dividing line or something like that. So
[521:54] what happens is that in that as you
[521:57] increase the K your bias basically
[522:02] increases but your variation reduces. So
[522:06] we know that in classification problems
[522:08] or in machine learning problems bias and
[522:11] variance are two things that we are
[522:13] trying to manage right bias is basically
[522:15] how close you are to the actual class or
[522:19] or to the actual uh value whereas
[522:22] variation is how much variability is
[522:24] there in your prediction. So uh as the k
[522:28] increases the bias sort of increases but
[522:31] the variance reduces and then it is vice
[522:33] versa. So if your K decreases let's say
[522:36] at K is equal to 1 where you are only
[522:39] looking at just one nearest neighbor and
[522:41] then predicting based on that actually
[522:44] the bias is the least which means it is
[522:46] the most flexible K is equal to 1 is the
[522:49] will give you the most flexible uh sort
[522:52] of demarketing line or function whereas
[522:56] the variability will be the maximum. So
[522:58] that that's the sort of the trade-off
[523:01] and that's how we actually determine K.
[523:04] So we have to get a K value in such a
[523:06] way based on trial and error that sort
[523:09] of maximizes our uh sort of or reduces
[523:12] the bias as well as the variance and and
[523:14] that's the kind of optimization we are
[523:16] trying to do. Okay. So how do we

[563:45] the given data set. Now to select the
[563:48] maximum hyper plane in the given sets,
[563:50] the support vector machine follows the
[563:52] following. It generates a hyper plane
